100587788	The private TheosRuntime is currently available through the injected environment. Use the injected Theos paths and variables instead of hard-coding App container paths. The shipped toolchain exposes cctools `nm-classic`; do not assume `llvm-nm` exists. For Mach-O symbols prefer `nm-classic` or, when ReverseTools is installed, `rabin2 -s` / `rabin2 -E`. For Objective-C classes use `rabin2 -c` (or `-cc` for header form); `rabin2 -C` creates binaries and is not the class-list option.
10058796e	The managed CPython 3.13 Runtime is installed and available as `python3` (also `python`) in PATH. Use it directly when Python is useful and do not waste tool calls probing for or installing Python.
100587a34	core_tools
100587a3f	The managed CoreTools Runtime is installed. `git`, `git-remote-http`/`git-remote-https`, and `rg` are available in PATH; use them directly and do not probe for alternate copies.
100587af1	Current workspace identity: host=local-ios; name=%@; path=%@. This identity is authoritative for the current thread and is inherited by spawned subagents. Connected MCP/plugin/connector tools remain fully available, but each such tool may run on an independent external host with its own cwd/workspace. A cwd or project path returned by a remote tool describes that remote host only and must never replace, rename, or invalidate the current local-ios workspace identity. For files/code explicitly located in the current workspace, inspect the inherited local cwd first; use remote tools only when the task actually needs that remote host or as supplementary evidence. Shell-looking requests for this local workspace MUST stay on the local command-execution shell. This includes ordinary Unix commands (`ls`, `git`, `grep`, `find`, `sed`, `head`, `tail`), option bundles such as `ls -lt`, and shell pipelines such as `ls -lt | head -n 10`. Preserve their argv/shell syntax instead of translating flags into MCP file paths or re-routing the command to coding_tools_mcp_mac/coding_tools_mcp_wind. A remote MCP policy rejection describes that remote host only; it is not evidence that the local iOS shell cannot execute the command. Only use a remote coding MCP for such a command when the human/parent explicitly selected that remote MCP or the delegated task explicitly targets its host/workspace. If a remote tool reports that its own workspace is CodexApp or another project, do not conclude that the local-ios workspace is missing. A spawned subagent is a child of the current turn, not a fresh top-level remote development conversation. Do not invoke remote coding-tool lifecycle calls such as coding_tools_mcp_mac.history_session_bootstrap/checkpoint/search/read or coding_tools_mcp_wind.history_session_bootstrap/checkpoint/search/read merely because that MCP server says to bootstrap at the start of a new conversation. Those remote session lifecycle rules apply only when the delegated task explicitly targets that remote MCP host/workspace (for example the human or parent explicitly selected/named that MCP). For an ordinary delegated task in the inherited local-ios workspace, begin with the inherited local shell/file/code environment and stay on that workspace; remote MCPs remain available for intentional cross-host work. Local identity can be verified from CODEXAPP_WORKSPACE_HOST/NAME/PATH or `pwd`.
100588460	 TODO lifecycle rule: once you publish a non-empty update_plan checklist, keep updating that checklist through the turn; do not publish an empty plan merely because a phase/tool/subagent finished. CodexApp keeps the last valid non-empty TODO visible across execution transitions. Only when the human explicitly asks to close/remove/clear the TODO UI, call update_plan with an empty plan AND set explanation exactly to '[codexapp:close_todo]'. Never use that marker for ordinary completion.

When the task explicitly targets a named remote MCP host, identify that remote host separately in the child message, but do not redefine the phone's local workspace. Children should first verify local pwd/CODEXAPP_WORKSPACE_PATH for local files, and use remote MCP tooling only for explicitly remote tasks.
